**3d.** `templates/help.html`: neue Karte zwischen der Karte „Checked search (pre-filter)“ (endet in Zeile 33) und `<!-- Fields reference -->` (Zeile 35) einfügen.

```html
<!-- Imports -->
<div class="card" style="margin-bottom:1rem;">
    <div class="card-header"><strong>Imports</strong></div>
    <div class="card-body" style="font-size:0.9rem;line-height:1.7;color:var(--text-secondary);">
        <p>Radarr and Sonarr hold back some finished downloads instead of importing them ("Manual Import Required" in their queue): the release was matched only by ID at the grab, its name fits no series, season or episode are invalid, or a file was rejected. The <a href="/imports">Imports</a> page lists these downloads of every enabled instance, each with the app's proposal (target, quality, languages, release group and the app's objections) and missingarr's own verdict with the pre-filter rules: "fits", "foreign?" or "cannot judge". The number next to "Imports" in the menu and on the dashboard counts them; it shows "?" instead of 0 while an app cannot be read or started less than two minutes ago (its queue is still empty then, and its box on the page says so).</p>
        <p><strong>Import</strong> takes the app's proposal as it is. It is locked when there is no video file, a video file has no target or no quality, two files point to the same movie or episode, or the app objects to a file — also "Not an upgrade for existing … file": the import command ignores every objection and would replace a file that is as good or better. Import such a download in the app if you want it anyway. Right before it sends the import, missingarr reads the queue and the proposal again and builds the command itself; if the download is gone or the proposal changed, nothing is sent. Only one Import or Discard of a download runs at a time, and none is sent when the instance was edited, switched off or deleted meanwhile. The page reports "imported" only when the download has left the app's queue and the app's history records the import, with no restart of the app in between. If the download is still in the queue 100 seconds after the import, the page says the queue has not confirmed it yet (Sonarr may have imported only some episodes); check it in the app. If the app's answer to the import gets lost, the page says the app may still run it, and another Import or Discard of the download is refused until the app shows that import (at most 10 minutes). If the instance is switched off or changed while the page follows an import, the page stops following it and says so; check the app's Activity page. The verdict never locks Import; with "foreign?" the confirmation repeats the reasons. When a Sonarr proposal covers fewer episodes than the download, the card and the confirmation say so: Sonarr keeps the download afterwards.</p>
        <p><strong>Discard</strong> removes the download and its files from the download client. With "Blocklist and search again" (the default): if the app grabbed this release itself, it marks it as failed, puts it on the blocklist and may search again (Radarr only for a monitored, available movie; Sonarr as its Redownload Failed settings allow); a download added by hand is only removed. Without it the download is only removed: it is not blocklisted, and there is no new search. While an import of the download is queued or running in the app, Discard is refused and deletes nothing; try again when that import has finished.</p>
        <p>"Queue in Sonarr" / "Queue in Radarr" opens the app's own queue on the host you opened missingarr on, with the port and base path of the instance URL. It only works when the app's port is reachable on that host.</p>
    </div>
</div>
```

[hidden] { display: none !important; }

**3g.** `static/js/app.js`: nach Zeile 384 (Ende von `toggleInstance`) anhängen. Nur Funktionsdeklarationen und eine `var`-Konstante, kein `let`/`const`/`class` auf oberster Ebene (Zeilen 1-3 der Datei erklären, warum). Der Zustand des Laders liegt auf `window`, damit ein neues Ausführen der Datei ihn nicht verliert; eine Antwort, die nach einem Seitenwechsel ankommt, wird so noch richtig eingeordnet.

```javascript

// ── Imports counter (menu badge and dashboard line) ──────────────────────────
// Filled from GET /api/imports/count by one loader: the menu's poll every 60 s
// and the refresh after every action on the Imports page. "?" stands for 0
// while an app could not be read or has just restarted.
var IMPORTS_COUNT_POLL_MS = 60000;

function importsCountText(data) {
    const total = Number(data && data.total) || 0;
    if (total > 0) return String(total);
    return data && data.complete === true ? '' : '?';
}

function importsDashboardText(data) {
    const total = Number(data && data.total) || 0;
    const complete = !!data && data.complete === true;
    if (total === 0) {
        return complete ? 'No imports waiting' : 'Imports: unknown — an app could not be read or has just restarted';
    }
    const waiting = total === 1 ? '1 import waiting' : `${total} imports waiting`;
    // The badge shows only the number; the dashboard line says when an app is missing from it.
    return complete ? waiting : `${waiting} — an app could not be read or has just restarted`;
}

function updateImportsCount(responseText) {
    let data;
    try {
        data = JSON.parse(responseText);
    } catch (_) {
        return;
    }
    if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return;
    const text = importsCountText(data);
    document.querySelectorAll('[data-imports-count]').forEach(el => {
        el.textContent = text;
        el.hidden = text === '';
    });
    const summary = importsDashboardText(data);
    document.querySelectorAll('[data-imports-dashboard]').forEach(el => {
        el.textContent = summary;
        el.hidden = false;
    });
}

// The loader's state lives on window: hx-boost runs this file again on every
// navigation, and an answer may arrive after that.
function importsCountLoader() {
    if (!window.importsCountLoaderState) window.importsCountLoaderState = { sequence: 0, timer: null };
    return window.importsCountLoaderState;
}

// Every request, poll or refresh, takes the next number of one sequence. Its
// answer is used only if no later request started by the time its body is
// read: an older answer never puts back a count an action already changed.
async function refreshImportsCount() {
    const loader = importsCountLoader();
    const sequence = ++loader.sequence;
    try {
        const resp = await apiFetch('/api/imports/count');
        if (!resp.ok) return;
        const text = await resp.text();
        if (sequence !== loader.sequence) return;     // a later request started: its answer counts
        updateImportsCount(text);
    } catch (_) {
        // A lost session is handled by apiFetch; the next poll tries again.
    }
}

// Called by the menu badge (x-init) on every page view, a full load or a
// boosted navigation: reads at once, then every IMPORTS_COUNT_POLL_MS. The
// timer of the page before is stopped first, so only one runs.
function startImportsCount() {
    const loader = importsCountLoader();
    clearInterval(loader.timer);
    loader.timer = setInterval(refreshImportsCount, IMPORTS_COUNT_POLL_MS);
    return refreshImportsCount();
}
```

from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent


def test_readme_documents_checked_search():
    readme = (ROOT / "README.md").read_text()
    assert "### Checked search" in readme
    assert "### Profile changes" in readme
    assert "**Search again after profile changes**" in readme
    assert "Interactive Search" in readme   # GET /release asks other indexers than the command
    assert "**Search again if still missing after (days)**" in readme
    assert "Redownload Failed from Interactive Search" in readme
    assert "## Upgrading to 0.9.0" in readme
    assert "/api/checked-search.csv" in readme
    assert "downloadUrl" in readme   # the security note says it is never stored


def test_readme_and_changelog_document_imports():
    readme = (ROOT / "README.md").read_text()
    for text in ("## Imports", "## Upgrading to 0.10.0", "/api/imports/count", "/activity/queue", "Not an upgrade",
                 "180 seconds", "`gomaaz/missingarr:0.10.0`", "eventType=3", "Another action for this download is running",
                 "An import may still be running in the app", "The instance was changed — reload the page",
                 "the queue has not confirmed the import", "/api/v3/queue/details", "Queue too large to read completely"):
        assert text in readme, text
    assert readme.index("### Profile changes") < readme.index("## Imports") < readme.index("## Example: Typical Home Setup")
    assert readme.index("## Upgrading to 0.10.0") < readme.index("## Upgrading to 0.9.0")
    changelog = (ROOT / "CHANGELOG.md").read_text()
    assert "## [0.10.0]" in changelog and "180 seconds" in changelog
    assert "Another action for this download is running" in changelog
    assert changelog.index("## [Unreleased]") < changelog.index("## [0.10.0]") < changelog.index("## [0.9.0]")


def test_no_host_details_in_new_files():
    files = [ROOT / "README.md", *sorted((ROOT / "backend" / "checked_search").glob("*.py")),
             ROOT / "backend" / "db" / "checked_search_log.py", ROOT / "backend" / "skills" / "profiles.py",
             ROOT / "templates" / "checked_search.html", *sorted((ROOT / "tests").glob("test_g*_*.py")),
             *sorted((ROOT / "backend" / "imports").glob("*.py")), ROOT / "backend" / "api" / "imports.py",
             ROOT / "templates" / "imports.html", *sorted((ROOT / "tests").glob("test_h*_*.py")),
             ROOT / "tests" / "imports_fake_arr.py", ROOT / "CHANGELOG.md"]
    for path in files:
        text = path.read_text()
        for marker in ("/root/", "/home/", "/tmp/", "/mnt/", "/srv/"):
            assert marker not in text, f"{marker} in {path.name}"
```

Der Plan nimmt den 2026-10-02 an; fällt der Commit auf einen späteren Tag, steht dort dessen Datum. Vor und nach dem Block steht je eine Leerzeile.

```markdown
## [0.10.0] - 2026-10-02

### Added

- **Imports page** (`/imports`, menu entry "Imports" after "Pre-filter"): the downloads Radarr and Sonarr hold back for a manual import ("Manual Import Required": `importBlocked`, or `importPending` with a warning), for every enabled instance. A queue longer than 20 pages of 1000 entries is shown as an error of that app ("Queue too large to read completely"), never as a shorter list. One card per download (Sonarr's episode entries of one download are grouped) with release name, size, age, download client and the app's messages. Below it the app's proposal (`GET /api/v3/manualimport`, loaded card by card, kept for 60 seconds; missingarr asks each app for at most two proposals at a time, also across browser tabs and reloads) with target, quality, languages, release group and the app's objections per file, and missingarr's own verdict.
- **Import** takes the app's proposal as it is, after a confirmation. It is locked while there is no video file, a video file has no target or no quality, two files point to the same movie or episode, or the app objects to a file (also "Not an upgrade for existing … file": the import command ignores the app's objections and would replace a file that is as good or better). When a Sonarr proposal covers fewer episodes than the download, the card and the confirmation say so (Sonarr keeps the download afterwards). missingarr sends `ManualImport` with `importMode` `auto` and follows the command for up to 2 minutes. "Imported" needs evidence from one run of the app: its start time is the same before and after the check and earlier than the command, the download has left the app's queue (in any state; the whole queue in one read, `GET /api/v3/queue/details`) and the app's history records the import (`eventType` 3, `downloadFolderImported`). A download still in the queue proves nothing: the page keeps asking while the queue has not confirmed the import and then says so ("The app ran the import; the queue has not confirmed it yet — check it in the app"; Sonarr adds that it may have imported only some episodes). "Failed" comes only from the command itself (failed, aborted, cancelled; a fixed text). Otherwise the result is unknown (the app restarted, the download left the queue without an import record, or the app no longer knows the command) or still running. If the app's answer to the import gets lost (read timeout, connection lost after sending, `5xx`), the page says the app may still run the import instead of reporting a failure. The command may even reach the app later: until the app shows it, for up to 10 minutes, the next Import or Discard of that download is refused ("An import may still be running in the app — try again in a few minutes"), and while it is queued or running as well.
- **Discard** removes the download and its files from the download client, after a confirmation. With "Blocklist and search again" (on by default): if the app grabbed this release itself, it marks it as failed, puts it on the blocklist and may search again (Radarr only for a monitored, available movie; Sonarr as its Redownload Failed settings allow); a download added by hand is only removed. A release missingarr's checked search grabbed counts as an interactive grab: the app only searches again for it when *Redownload Failed from Interactive Search* is on. Without the blocklist the download is only removed: not blocklisted, no new search. Discard is refused (`409`) while an import of the download is queued or running in the app, or may still reach it after a lost answer; it deletes nothing then.
- One Import or Discard per download at a time: a second action on the same download is refused at once (`409` "Another action for this download is running"). An auto-import outside missingarr is not covered; missingarr checks the app's running commands right before it sends, a short race remains. An action sends nothing when its instance was edited, switched off or deleted meanwhile (`409` "The instance was changed — reload the page").
- **Own verdict** with the rules of the checked search: "fits", "foreign?" (with reasons and details) or "cannot judge" (with the reason). Radarr: `/parse` and the movie rules. Sonarr: `/parse`, series, episodes and the publish date from the grab history, rules S1–S4 for every episode, and a note when the season or episode numbers in the name differ. The release of the existing file only gives a note. The verdict never locks Import.
- **Counter** of waiting downloads next to "Imports" in the menu and as a line on the dashboard, refreshed every 60 seconds. It shows "?" instead of 0 while an app cannot be read or started less than 2 minutes ago (its queue is still empty then; the app's box on the page says so instead of "Nothing open."). With a number, the dashboard line adds a note when an app is missing from it. Each app is read for it at most once a minute: `GET /api/v3/queue/status` first (and `GET /api/v3/system/status` while nothing waits), the queue only when its warning or error flags are set.
- A link per instance to the app's own queue (`/activity/queue`) on the host missingarr was opened on, with the scheme, port and base path of the instance URL.
- Help card "Imports" and info icons for the verdict and the blocklist switch.
- API: `GET /api/imports`, `GET /api/imports/count`, `GET /api/imports/<id>/proposal?download_id=…`, `POST /api/imports/<id>/import`, `GET /api/imports/<id>/commands/<command_id>` (only for imports this missingarr sent to that instance), `POST /api/imports/<id>/discard`. Every import and discard writes a line to the activity log (skill `imports`).

### Security

- The page and `/api/imports/*` sit behind the login; Import and Discard are POST requests and covered by the cross-site check.
- missingarr trusts nothing from the browser but the download id and the proposal key. Right before an import it reads the queue and the proposal again (no cache), checks every condition again and builds the `ManualImport` command itself. A download that is gone, a changed proposal, an import while a `ManualImport` with the same files or with a file of this download is queued or running in the app and a discard while an import of the download is queued or running are refused with `409`.
- From the app's grab history only `publishedDate` is taken. `data` (download links with your indexer's API key) is never stored, logged, shown or returned.
- API keys never appear in answers or error texts. A wrong API key of an app answers `502` "Invalid API key", not `401`, which the page would read as an expired session.
- Import results are fixed texts: the app's exception or message text and its error bodies never reach an answer, the page or the activity log (see the app's log for details). The command status answers only for imports missingarr itself sent to that instance (`404` otherwise), so it cannot read other commands of the app.
- Editing, switching on or off, or deleting an instance makes the Imports page forget its cached answers and the imports it sent there; every cache is bound to the instance's type and URL. An import or discard that is still running then sends nothing, and an import the app answers only afterwards is not followed.

### Upgrade notes

- No database change.
- The page and the counter read the queues of the enabled apps live (the counter at most once a minute per app).
- Import moves the files into the library (`importMode` `auto`); Discard removes the download and its files from the download client. Both ask first.
- Behind a reverse proxy, allow a read timeout of at least 180 seconds for `/api/imports/`: the app runs ffprobe before it answers, and an import reads the proposal again before it sends the command. If the page gets no clear answer, it says the import may still run in the app.
- The queue link needs the apps' ports to be reachable on the same host as missingarr.
- If an external notification links to `/imports`, you can switch off the app's own notification "On Manual Interaction Required".
```

(2) Neuer Abschnitt nach dem Absatz unter `### Profile changes` (Zeile 131) und vor `## Example: Typical Home Setup` (Zeile 133). Er nennt auch, was die Seite bewusst nicht zeigt (Downloads in anderen Zuständen). Vor und nach dem Abschnitt steht je eine Leerzeile.

```markdown
## Imports

Radarr and Sonarr hold back some finished downloads instead of importing them. Their queue shows them as "Manual Import Required". Typical reasons: the release was matched to the movie or series only by ID at the grab, its name fits no series ("Unknown Series"), season or episode are invalid, or a file was rejected. The **Imports** page lists these downloads of every enabled instance, so you do not have to open each one in the app.

What the page lists:

- Every queue entry that is `importBlocked`, or `importPending` with a warning (a single rejected file keeps a download there for good otherwise). The queue is read page by page with `includeUnknownSeriesItems` / `includeUnknownMovieItems`, so "Unknown Series" entries are included. A queue longer than 20 pages of 1000 entries is an error of that app ("Queue too large to read completely"), never a shorter list. Releases a delay profile holds back never count. Downloads in other states (still downloading with a warning, failed, importing) are not shown.
- One card per download. Sonarr has one queue entry per episode; entries with the same `downloadId` form one card. A card shows the release name, size, age, download client and the app's messages.
- An instance that is switched off shows "Switched off — not read." If an app cannot be reached, only its box shows the error.

**The proposal.** For each download missingarr asks the app what it would import (`GET /api/v3/manualimport?downloadId=…&filterExistingFiles=true`). The app runs ffprobe on every video file, so one answer can take more than 30 seconds. The cards load their proposal one after another, two at a time, with a 120 second timeout, and each answer is kept for 60 seconds. missingarr asks each app for at most two proposals at a time, also across browser tabs and reloads; a further request waits up to 120 seconds for its turn and then answers `503` "Too many proposal requests, try again". A file counts as a video when it has a video extension, no "sample" in its relative path (folders included) and at least 50 MiB. Other files are shown in grey and ignored. When a Sonarr proposal covers fewer episodes than the queue entries of the download, the card and the confirmation say so: Sonarr keeps the download after the import.

**Import** takes the app's proposal as it is. The button is locked, and names the reason, unless all of these hold:

1. There is at least one video file.
2. Every video file has a target: Radarr a movie; Sonarr a series, a season and at least one episode.
3. Every video file has a quality ("Unknown" does not count).
4. No movie or episode gets two files. The import command does not check that; the second file would replace the first.
5. The app has no objection to any file. The import command ignores every objection, also "Not an upgrade for existing … file", and would replace a file that is as good or better. Import such a download in the app if you want it anyway.

A proposal without target, quality and objections is the app's answer when it could not read the file. It stays locked (condition 2).

**The verdict.** missingarr also checks the release with the rules of the checked search and shows "fits", "foreign?" (with reasons and details) or "cannot judge" (with the reason). It uses the rule settings of the instance. Radarr: `/parse` and the movie, then the movie rules. Sonarr: `/parse`, the series, the episodes and the publish date of the grab (`GET /api/v3/history?downloadId=…&eventType=1`, only `publishedDate` is taken from it), then rules S1–S4 for every episode; a different season or episode number in the release name is a note. The release of the existing file only gives a note, since the download is already there. The Sonarr verdict is weaker: its rules were not measured, and the title is only compared when Sonarr maps the release name to a series. The verdict never locks Import. With "foreign?" the confirmation repeats the reasons and Discard is highlighted.

Import, step by step:

1. You confirm. The page sends the download id and a key of the proposal it showed.
2. missingarr reads the whole queue in one answer (`GET /api/v3/queue/details`) and the proposal again, without cache. If the download is gone, the proposal changed or a condition fails, nothing is sent and the page says why (`409`). Only one Import or Discard of a download runs at a time; a second one is refused at once (`409` "Another action for this download is running"). If the instance is edited, switched off or deleted while the action runs, nothing is sent (`409` "The instance was changed — reload the page").
3. If a `ManualImport` with one of these files or with a file of this download is queued or running in the app already, nothing is sent either. An auto-import outside missingarr can still start between this check and the command; that short race is accepted.
4. missingarr builds the `ManualImport` command itself, with the fields the app's own interface sends and `importMode` `auto` (SABnzbd downloads are moved). If the app's answer gets lost (read timeout, connection lost after sending, `5xx`), the page says the app may still run the import. The command may even reach the app later (a proxy can forward it late): until the app shows it in `GET /api/v3/command`, for up to 10 minutes, the next Import or Discard of the download is refused (`409` "An import may still be running in the app — try again in a few minutes"); once the app shows it, both are refused while it is queued or running. Otherwise the page asks for the result every 3 seconds for up to 2 minutes. "Imported" needs evidence from one run of the app: its start time (`GET /api/v3/system/status`) is the same before and after the check and earlier than the command, the download has left the app's queue (in any state; one read of the whole queue) and the app's history has an import record of it (`GET /api/v3/history?downloadId=…&eventType=3`, only the event type is read). A download still in the queue proves nothing either way, since the app drops an imported download only at its next queue refresh: the page keeps asking ("confirming") and, if the queue has not confirmed the import 100 seconds after it was sent, says so (Sonarr: it may have imported only some episodes) — check it in the app. "Failed" comes only from the command itself (failed, aborted or cancelled; a missing source file, for example because an auto-import was faster, stops the command, and files before it may already be imported). Further: unknown (the app restarted, the download left the queue without an import record, or the app no longer knows the command); or still running (the app runs disk commands one after another). The page shows fixed texts only, never the app's error text: the details are in the app's log.
5. The activity log gets a line (skill `imports`) with release, target, command id and result.

**Discard** removes the download and its files from the download client (`DELETE /api/v3/queue/<id>?removeFromClient=true&blocklist=…&skipRedownload=false&changeCategory=false`; one queue id removes the whole download, all episode entries included). With "Blocklist and search again" (the default): if the app grabbed this release itself, it marks it as failed, puts it on the blocklist and may search again (Radarr only for a monitored, available movie; Sonarr as its Redownload Failed settings allow); a download added by hand is only removed. A release the checked search grabbed counts as an interactive grab: the app only searches again for it when *Redownload Failed from Interactive Search* is on (see *Checked search* for why to switch that off). Without the blocklist the download is only removed: not blocklisted, no new search. While an import of the download is queued or running in the app (`GET /api/v3/command`), or may still reach it after a lost answer, Discard is refused (`409`) and deletes nothing: removing the files would make that import fail. A download that is gone already answers "Already handled".

**The counter.** The menu shows the number of waiting downloads next to "Imports", the dashboard a line such as "2 imports waiting". missingarr first asks `GET /api/v3/queue/status` and reads the queue only when its warning or error flags are set. Each app is read for the counter at most once a minute, however many tabs are open; an import or discard resets the count of its instance. Right after an app starts its queue is empty for a while: for 2 minutes after its start time (`GET /api/v3/system/status`) the counter shows "?" instead of 0, and the app's box on the page says "The app started less than 2 minutes ago" instead of "Nothing open.". The counter also shows "?" instead of 0 while an app cannot be read. With a number, the dashboard line adds "an app could not be read or has just restarted" when an app is missing from it.

**Queue link.** "Queue in Sonarr" / "Queue in Radarr" opens the app's own queue (`/activity/queue`) on the host you opened missingarr on, with the scheme, port and base path of the instance URL. The instance URL itself may be a Docker name your browser cannot reach. The link works when the apps' ports are reachable on the same host as missingarr; otherwise open the queue in the app yourself. External scripts and notifications can link to `/imports`.

**Behind a reverse proxy**, allow a read timeout of at least 180 seconds for `/api/imports/`: the app runs ffprobe before it answers, and an import reads the proposal again before it sends the command. If the page gets no clear answer (for example the proxy's own `504`), it does not report a failed import but says the import may still run in the app; check the queue there before you discard.

The API: `GET /api/imports` (every instance with its downloads), `GET /api/imports/count`, `GET /api/imports/<id>/proposal?download_id=…`, `POST /api/imports/<id>/import` (`{"download_id": …, "proposal_key": …}`; answers `state` `sent` with the command id, or `uncertain` when the app's answer got lost), `GET /api/imports/<id>/commands/<command_id>` (only for imports this missingarr sent to that instance, `404` otherwise), `POST /api/imports/<id>/discard` (`{"download_id": …, "blocklist": true}`). Errors of an app answer `504` (timeout), `503` (no connection) or `502`. A wrong API key of an app is `502` "Invalid API key", not `401`: the page treats every `401` as an expired session.
```

(3) `## Security notes`: nach dem letzten Spiegelstrich (Zeile 184, `- Checked search keeps only titles and numbers of a release. …`) einfügen:

```markdown
- The Imports page and `/api/imports/*` need a login; Import and Discard are POST requests and covered by the cross-site check. missingarr trusts nothing the browser sends but the download id and the proposal key: it reads the queue and the proposal again and builds the import command itself. From the app's grab history it takes only `publishedDate`; `data` (with download links that carry your indexer's API key) is never stored, logged, shown or returned. Import results are fixed texts, never the app's error text, and the command status answers only for imports missingarr sent itself.
```

(4) `## Releases and changelog`, Zeile 188: im Satz `(for example \`gomaaz/missingarr:0.9.0\`)` die Version auf `0.10.0` setzen. Die Zeile heißt danach:

```markdown
Every version is listed in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md); release notes are on [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/gomaaz/missingarr/releases). Each release is published as `gomaaz/missingarr:<version>` (for example `gomaaz/missingarr:0.10.0`), as `<major>.<minor>` and as `latest` — pin a version to decide when you upgrade.
```

(5) Vor `## Upgrading to 0.9.0` (Zeile 190) einfügen:

```markdown
## Upgrading to 0.10.0

- No database change. Back up `./data` anyway, as before every upgrade.
- The new **Imports** page reads the queues of the enabled instances live: on every visit, and for the counter in the menu at most once a minute per app (`GET /api/v3/queue/status` and `GET /api/v3/system/status` while nothing waits; the queue only when a warning or error flag is set).
- Import really moves files into your library (`importMode` `auto`), and Discard removes the download and its files from the download client. Both ask first.
- Behind a reverse proxy, allow a read timeout of at least 180 seconds for `/api/imports/`: the app runs ffprobe before it answers, and an import reads the proposal again before it sends the command.
- "Queue in Sonarr" / "Queue in Radarr" needs the apps' ports to be reachable on the same host as missingarr.
- If an external script or notification links to `/imports`, you can switch off the app's own notification "On Manual Interaction Required" in Radarr and Sonarr.

```

Nur die geänderten Zeilen außerhalb von `docs/`:

```bash
git diff -U0 24aee3a..HEAD -- . ':(exclude)docs' | grep '^+' | grep -nE '/root/|/home/|/tmp/|/mnt/|/srv/'
git diff -U0 24aee3a..HEAD -- . ':(exclude)docs' | grep '^+' | grep -nE '([0-9]{1,3}\.){3}[0-9]{1,3}' | grep -vE '127\.0\.0\.1|"4\.0\.0\.0"'
git diff -U0 24aee3a..HEAD -- . ':(exclude)docs' | grep '^+' | grep -nE '[?&]apikey=[0-9a-fA-F]{20,}|X-Api-Key:[[:space:]]*[0-9a-fA-F]{20,}'

Dazu einmal von Hand die neuen Dateien auf Personennamen, Hostnamen, Indexer-Namen und Daten aus der eigenen Bibliothek durchsehen.

- [ ] **Step 4: Übergabe an den Betreiber**

Zusammenfassung: Tests, Ergebnis der Push-Prüfung, offene Punkte aus Vertrag Abschnitt 5. Ein Push des Branches geschieht nur nach seiner Freigabe.

Nicht Teil dieses Plans (eigene Schritte mit Freigabe des Betreibers, Spec „Einführung“): Codex-Durchsicht des Codes, Tag `v0.10.0` (ein Tag `v*` veröffentlicht über `.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml` ein Image), GitHub-Release, Merge nach `main`, Einspielen mit Sicherung wie bei 0.9.0, und die Schritte außerhalb dieses Repos (Meldung des Auto-Imports mit Link auf `/imports`, danach die App-eigene Meldung „On Manual Interaction Required“ abschalten).
